## NCT07162116
A Single-center, Randomized, Double-blind, Placebo- and Positive Drug (Open-label Design) Controlled Phase I Clinical Study to Evaluate the Effect of a Single Oral Dose of XY0206 Tablets on QTc Interval in Healthy Chinese Participants.

Intervention: : XY0206 — 12.5 mg/table,On the morning of the day of dosing , subjects received XY0206 tablets orally on an empty stomach.Each group of 15 subjects was randomly assigned in a 3:1:1 ratio, with 9 receiving XY0206, 3 receiving a placebo of XY0206, and 3 receiving moxifloxacin.Group A (XY0206 tablets: 37.5 mg; moxifloxacin : 400 mg; placebo: 37.5 mg)；Group B(XY0206 tablets: 75 mg; moxifloxacin : 400 mg; placebo: 150mg)；Group C(XY0206 tablets:150 mg; moxifloxacin : 400 mg; placebo: 150mg)
: Moxifloxacin (400 mg) — 400mg，On the morning of the day of dosing , subjects received moxifloxacin orally on an empty stomach
: XY0206 Placebo — 12.5mg/table,On the morning of the day of dosing, subjects received XY0206 placebo orally on an empty stomach.Each group of 15 subjects was randomly assigned in a 3:1:1 ratio, with 9 receiving XY0206, 3 receiving a placebo of XY0206, and 3 receiving moxifloxacin.Group A (XY0206 tablets: 37.5 mg; moxifloxacin : 400 mg; placebo: 37.5 mg)；Group B(XY0206 tablets: 75 mg; moxifloxacin : 400 mg; placebo: 150mg)；Group C(XY0206 tablets:150 mg; moxifloxacin : 400 mg; placebo: 150mg)
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07162116

## NCT07603674
Precision-based Care for People With Musculoskeletal Conditions Seeking Primary Health Care: Enhancing Recovery and Health Outcomes

Intervention: : No intervention — The patients are followed over 6 month through patient reported outcome measures. No intervention other than the regular treatment that the physiotherapists give. In this project the participating physiotherapists are not instructed to treat the participating patients according to the recommended treatment level based on the risk group. The physiotherapists are instructed to use the Start MSK the first visit and to use the results in the dialogue with the patients. The investigators will follow up on what treatment that is given with a short survey at 8 weeks. This survey comprises questions on how many treatment sessions were given, and include a multiple choice question with a list of possible treatments (exercise, information, home exercise, mobilisation, manipulation, stretching, modalities etc). The physiotherapists report treatment given by checking boxes of the different treatments. This information will later be analysed in relation to risk group (low, medium and high risk).
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07603674

SUMMARY:

## NCT07909044
The Vendor Business School for Healthy Diets (VBS-4HD): Food Vendors as Agents of Better Urban Nutrition

Intervention: : Training, coaching and marketing program — The VBS-4HD will be a 8-months program divided into three main components:

1. Group training (awareness and knowledge creation): fifteen 1.5-hour sessions offered twice per week for a total of approximately 15 sessions. Trainings will be offered in groups of approx. 8-12 vendors. Each group/cohort will be assigned a dedicated and extensively trained facilitator.
2. Individual coaching (empowerment for action): After completion of the group training sessions, the program will offer 3-4 individual coaching sessions. As much as possible, vendors will be coached by the same facilitator that trained them. Vendors will receive one coaching session of about 1hour every two weeks.
3. Food marketing campaign (increasing demand for nutritious foods): One month after starting the coaching phase, vendors will be enrolled to participate in a vendor-driven food marketing campaign developed by marketing experts.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07909044

SUMMARY:

## NCT07909538
The Effectiveness of Structured Swallowing Exercise Therapy in Patients With Nasopharyngeal Carcinoma: An Assessment of Swallowing Function and Quality of Life

Intervention: : Structured Swallowing Exercise — The structured swallowing exercise program is a multimodal, systematic, and progressive swallowing rehabilitation program consisting of Isometric Neck Flexion-Extension Exercise, Jaw Opening Exercise, Jaw Range of Motion Exercise, Tongue Range of Motion Exercise, Chin Tuck Against Resistance (CTAR), Mendelsohn Maneuver, and Diaphragmatic Breathing Exercise. The exercises are organized progressively into preparation, strengthening, coordination, and functional phases. The program is delivered for 30 minutes per session, twice weekly at the hospital for 8 weeks, with additional home exercises three times weekly. Individual modifications may be made according to the participant's clinical condition, and adherence is monitored through an exercise logbook and periodic supervision.
: Conventional Swallowing Exercise — The conventional swallowing exercise program consists of Chin Tuck Against Resistance (CTAR) and Isometric Neck Flexion-Extension Exercise, primarily targeting suprahyoid and anterior cervical muscle strengthening and hyolaryngeal excursion. CTAR includes 3 cycles of 60-second isometric exercise and 2 cycles of 30 repetitions of isotonic exercise, repeated for 3 sets per day. Isometric Neck Flexion-Extension Exercise consists of resisted neck flexion and extension, with each contraction maintained for 5-10 seconds, performed in 3 sets per day with 10-15 repetitions per set. The program is delivered for 30 minutes per session, twice weekly at the hospital for 8 weeks, with additional home exercises three times weekly. Adherence is monitored during the study.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07909538

SUMMARY:

## NCT07910110
Neurobiological Markers of Non-suicidal Self-injury and Treatment Mechanisms of Adolescent Self-injury Intervention Skills Training

Intervention: : Adolescent Self-Injury Intervention Skills Training (ASIST) — ASIST is a structured psychological intervention developed for adolescents who engage in non-suicidal self-injury. The program consists of four sessions delivered over four weeks, focusing on: (1) understanding self-injury, behavioral chain analysis, and safety planning; (2) identifying and modifying automatic thoughts, core beliefs, and rumination; (3) improving emotional awareness, expression, and regulation; and (4) developing stress management, coping, and interpersonal communication skills. The intervention incorporates cognitive behavioral and emotion regulation strategies. Participants with persistent clinically significant difficulties at the four-week follow-up may receive two additional sessions addressing cognitive and interpretation biases and difficulties identifying and expressing emotions.
: Treatment as Usual (TAU) — Participants assigned to the TAU group will receive routine clinical care for non-suicidal self-injurious behavior over a 4-week period. Depending on their individual clinical needs, treatment may include psychoeducation, safety planning, behavioral chain analysis, counseling, and supportive psychotherapy, consistent with usual care practices in child and adolescent psychiatry.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07910110

## NCT07910812
Effects of a Tailored Intervention With Digital Health Tools on Sedentary Behaviour and Health-related Physical Outcomes in Long-term Cardiac Rehabilitation Patients: a Randomized Controlled Trial

Intervention: : Standard Cardiac Rehabilitation Care — Participants will receive usual care consisting of 60 minutes CR exercise sessions 2 or 3 times/week either online or in a face-to-face model (according to preference). Each exercise session starts with a 5'-10' minutes warm-up, followed by a 30'-45' minutes of combined training with an aerobic component - moderate intensity (40% to 69% of Heart Rate Reserve (HRR); 12-14 RPE) and a strength component - 8 to 10 exercises, 40-80% of 1-RM, 12-15 repetitions, 2 sets - ending with a 5'-10' minute cool down. Participants will have an individual consultation with an exercise physiologist to receive feedback over baseline assessments and SB/PA education (WHO guidelines) and will have access to educational videos on SB and PA that can be consulted at any time.
: Tailored behavioural counselling — Participants in the IG will receive six brief (10-15 min) individualized counselling sessions with an exercise physiologist at baseline, 1, 2, 3, 6, and 12 months. Sessions include feedback on assessments, education on WHO PA/SB guidelines, identification of barriers and facilitators, shared decision-making, goal setting and review, reinforcement of strategies, and maintenance planning. Counselling is supported by the official Brief Counselling Tools for Physical Activity Promotion (Directorate-General of Health, Portugal, 2018), including the Physical Activity Guide, Decision Aid, Action Plan, and Maintenance Plan. Participants are encouraged to use a smartphone application to self-monitor daily step count throughout the intervention. Self-monitoring supports behavioural strategies during the intensive (0-3 months) and moderate (3-6 months) phases, followed by a no-contact follow-up (6-12 months).
: Digital prompts — Participants in the IG will receive digital prompts throughout the intervention, delivered via SMS, WhatsApp, or email according to preference. During the first month, prompts are general, focusing on awareness, motivation, and reinforcement of PA/SB recommendations. After barriers and facilitators are identified in counselling sessions, prompts are tailored using the COM-B model, with Behavior Change Techniques such as goal setting, self-reward, social support, information on consequences, or restructuring barriers applied according to whether the challenge relates to Capability, Opportunity, or Motivation. Prompts are written to be concise, practical, and easy to integrate into daily routines. They are delivered weekly during the intensive phase (0-3 months) and biweekly during the moderate phase (3-6 months). No prompts are delivered during the follow-up phase (6-12 months).
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07910812

SUMMARY:

## NCT07910955
Effects of Balance Training on Balance Performance, Fall Risk, and Fear of Falling in Individuals With Glaucoma: A Randomized Controlled Trial

Intervention: : Balance Training Program — Participants allocated to the intervention group will participate in a structured balance training program for 8 weeks, three days per week, with each session lasting approximately 45 minutes. The program will include static and dynamic balance exercises, such as tandem stance, single-leg stance, tandem walking, heel-to-toe walking, directional changes, sit-to-stand exercises, and progressive postural control activities. Exercise difficulty will be progressively increased according to each participant's functional capacity and tolerance. All exercise sessions will be performed under supervision, with appropriate safety precautions to minimize the risk of falls. Participants will continue their routine glaucoma treatment throughout the study.

## NCT05272280
Ultrasound Guided External Oblique Intercostal (EOI) Block Versus Erector Spinae Plane Block (ESPB) for Postoperative Analgesia in Laparoscopic Cholecystectomy

Intervention: : External oblique intercostal (EOI) block — A high-frequency linear ultrasound probe will be placed in a longitudinal parasagittal orientation at the sixth intercostal space in the anterior midaxillary line. A 21G 10 cm needle will be inserted using an in plane approach. The tip of the needle will be placed into the fascial plane on the deep aspect of the external oblique muscle. A volume of 20 mL of LA mixture (10 mL of bupivacaine 0.5%, 5 mL of lidocaine 2% and 5 mL of normal saline to make a mixture totaling 20 ml.) will be injected. The same procedure will be repeated for the opposite side.
: Erector spinae plane block (ESPB) — A high-frequency linear ultrasound probe will be placed in a longitudinal parasagittal orientation 2.5-3 cm lateral to the T9 spinous process. The erector spinae muscles will be identified superficial to the tip of the T9 transverse process. A 21G 10 cm needle will be inserted using an in plane approach. The tip of the needle will be placed into the fascial plane on the deep aspect of the erector spinae muscle. The location of the needle tip will be confirmed by visible fluid spread lifting the erector spinae muscle off the bony shadow of the transverse process on ultrasonographic imaging. A volume of 20 mL of Local Anesthetic mixture (10 mL of bupivacaine 0.5%, 5 mL of lidocaine 2% and 5 mL of normal saline to make a mixture totaling 20 ml.) will be injected. The same procedure will be repeated for the opposite side.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT05272280

SUMMARY:

## NCT07066137
Motor Planning, Execution, and Modulation in Healthy Individuals and Patients With Movement Disorders or Neuromuscular Diseases Using Traditional Physical Activity or Advanced Devices to Enhance Motor Learning and Sensorimotor Processing

Intervention: : CAREN — The interventions include proprioceptive stimuli combined with specific rehabilitation protocols, such as Tabata training, plyometric training, and visual-cognitive-motor enhancement with the S.V.T.A. method.

Healthy subjects and athletes (about 50) will participate in intensive aerobic and anaerobic training, including sensory-motor-cognitive training and exercises on the CAREN platform. Another 25 participants will have TMS/tDCS and music therapy added to their regimen.

Regarding neurological patients, 50 participants will undergo posture and gait rehabilitation using the CAREN platform, while 25 will receive non-invasive neuromodulation to enhance their rehabilitation outcomes. This arm of the study focuses on the recovery of motor and cognitive functions.

This study uses the CAREN system as a tool for physical rehabilitation in neurological and healthy participants. The device is not being investigated for new indications and the study is conducted entirely in Italy.
: Control group — the control group is typically the group that does not receive the experimental treatment or intervention, serving as a baseline to compare the effects of the intervention. However, in the context you're describing with healthy subjects and athletes, here's how it's structured:

Healthy subjects and athletes (around 50 participants) will undergo:

Intensive aerobic and anaerobic training Sensory-motor-cognitive training

Additionally, 25 of these participants will receive:

Transcranial Magnetic Stimulation (TMS) Transcranial Direct Current Stimulation (tDCS) Music therapy This approach allows for a comparison between the effects of the physical and cognitive training alone versus the enhanced effects when combined with neuromodulation (TMS, tDCS) and music therapy. This design will help in assessing the added value of these interventions on performance
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07066137

SUMMARY:

## NCT07154329
Examining the Effect of Internet-Based EMDR Flash Intervention (SKY-Flash) on University Students' Test Anxiety Levels and User Experiences

Intervention: : Internet-Based EMDR-Flash Program — Self-guided, internet-based EMDR-Flash program (SKY-Flash) tailored to reduce test anxiety in university students. Delivered via a secure web platform with no live therapist involvement. The intervention comprises six \~40-minute modules completed independently during the study. Each module includes brief psychoeducation and step-by-step EMDR-Flash procedures (focus on a positive engaging image with brief "flash" cues), guided practice, and in-platform exercises. Gamification (progress tracking, badges) and automated prompts support adherence. Unlike CBT-based online programs or clinician-delivered EMDR, this is a fully automated, low-intensity Flash protocol adapted to exam-related distress. Safety messaging and referral resources are embedded; access is via individual login and usage is recorded for adherence.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07154329

SUMMARY:

Functional Improvement in Subacute/Chronic Stroke Patients Through a Non-invasive Virtual-reality Based Telerehabilitation Home System

Intervention: : Non- immersive virtual reality (niVR)-based telerehabilitation home program — Muvity (software of non-immersive virtual reality (niVR) through a depth camera installed in a personal computer (PC)) will be provided with a personalized treatment plan to be carried out 3times/week along 12w in the patient's home. The user will see exercises and serious games as daily quests, in a gamified environment with alerts to remind the scheduled treatment. The potential exercises include following a virtual avatar to perform individual movements (shoulder flexion-extension, shoulder vertical or horizontal abduction-adduction, elbow flexion-extension, hip flexion-extension or hip abduction-adduction), squats or medio-lateral movements of the pelvis to control the body weight transfer. The serious games are oriented toward performing movements in activities of daily living, such as cooking, cleaning a mirror, or acting as a goalkeeper, or collecting apples.

## NCT07260149
Coordinated Care for Alcohol Problems (CCAP): a Randomised Controlled Trial From Goa, India

Intervention: : CCAP — Hazardous drinking: The content of the intervention includes seven themes: health education, alcohol reduction, drinking and risk management, drinking alternatives, situational content, urge management, and relapse prevention. Participants will receive texts and video-based messages building their awareness about drinking and associated harms.

Harmful drinking: CAP has three phases which are delivered in one to four sessions over six to eight weeks by NSHWs. Each session lasts between 30 to 45 minutes. The content is delivered through motivational interviewing with additional behavioural and cognitive components. CAP+ will include treatment engagement strategies integrated into CAP.

Dependent drinking: Home detoxification will be supervised by a doctor and monitored by the NSHW and a designated carer (e.g. spouse) for the patient at their home-setting. The counselling sessions are delivered by NSHWs, over 4-8 weeks and lasting 30-60 minutes each.
: Enhanced usual care — Hazardous drinking: An information sheet/brochure with information on alcohol consumption, associated risks, and tips to manage and reduce drinking.

Harmful drinking: CAP psychological brief treatment delivered by trained NSHWs (described above in detail).

Dependent drinking: Hospital-based detoxification delivered in secondary or tertiary care.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07260149

SUMMARY:

## NCT07327723
The Effects of Repetitive Peripheral Magnetic Stimulation on Clinical and Electrophysiological Parameters in Patients With Carpal Tunnel Syndrome: A Double-Blind, Randomized, Sham-Controlled Trial

Intervention: : rPMS group — All patients will be instructed in tendon and nerve gliding exercises by a physiotherapist. In addition to these exercises, the treatment group will undergo repetitive peripheral magnetic stimulation (rPMS) therapy three times per week for two consecutive weeks, for a total of six sessions. Each session will last 10 minutes. The rPMS treatment will be administered using the BTL-6000 Super Inductive System Elite device. From the device interface, the "wrist/hand" region will be selected, and the "carpal tunnel syndrome" protocol pre-programmed for this region will be applied.

During the procedure, patients will be instructed to lie supine or sit in a relaxed position to ensure complete muscle relaxation in the arm. The applicator will be positioned on the volar aspect of the wrist, aligned with the carpal tunnel, as close to the skin as possible.

The frequency will be set between 5-50 Hz, and intensity will be adjusted to elicit visibly observable muscle contractions.
: sham-rPMS — In the sham group, patients will be positioned identically, and the device will be placed in the same position but not activated. Instead, previously recorded operational sounds of the device will be played back during the same duration to simulate the treatment experience.

All participants will be closely monitored throughout the treatment period
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07327723

SUMMARY:

## NCT07366892
Fluorescence Visualization and Image Quality Evaluation of Digital Visualization of Suspected Grade III and/or IV Gliomas During Neurosurgery (Leica GLOW400)

Intervention: : GLOW400 is a digital surgical microscope accessory used in fluorescent visualization of suspected grade III or IV gliomas during neurosurgery. — The digital surgical microscope accessory GLOW400 is using the optical excitation and emission filtering of the FL400. The FL400 provides an illumination filter (380nm - 430nm) which when engaged into the light source path of the surgical operating microscope provides, via a light guide, a fluorescence excitation light system used in conjunction with an approved tumor-selective substance (fluorophore) (e.g. Protoporphyrin IX (PpIX) which is resulting of the metabolization of 5-ALA (5 - aminolaevulinic acid)) for tissue characterization in the open neurosurgery field. PpIX is strongly fluorescent with a peak at λ=635 nm as red-violet after excitation with blue light (λ=380-410 nm). The digital surgical microscope accessory GLOW400 is using the optical excitation and emission filtering of the FL400. The fluorescence signal visualized depicts the distribution of the dye in the patient's tissues during the surgical procedure.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07366892

Postoperative Adjuvant Radiotherapy Strategy for Resectable Sinonasal Adenoid Cystic Carcinoma: A Prospective, Multicenter, Real-World Study

Intervention: : Postoperative Radiotherapy — Intensity-modulated radiotherapy (IMRT) or volumetric modulated arc therapy (VMAT) delivered to the postoperative tumor bed. High-risk clinical target volume (CTV1) receives 60-66 Gy in 30-33 fractions (1.8-2.0 Gy per fraction). Low-risk clinical target volume (CTV2) receives 54-60 Gy in 30-33 fractions. Treatment starts within 4-6 weeks after surgery. Daily image guidance is used. Organs at risk are constrained according to QUANTEC/RTOG guidelines
: Observation — No postoperative radiotherapy or any other form of adjuvant radiation. Patients undergo regular follow-up according to the study protocol, including physical examination, laboratory tests, and imaging (contrast-enhanced MRI of the head and neck every 6 months for the first 3 years and annually thereafter; chest CT annually). Active surveillance is performed to detect any recurrence or metastasis

## NCT07668661
OBEDIAM: a Pilot Study of Therapeutic Patient Education in Adults Living With Obesity, Diabetes, and Metabolic Disorders

Intervention: : Therapeutic patient education — The intervention consists of a patient education program. This program, structured in four phases.

1. Initially, a semi-structured individual interview is conducted with a nurse or physician to carry out a shared educational assessment.
2. Then a personalized educational program is co-created in the form of an educational contract.
3. The core of the intervention is a one-day in-person patient education session, offered to groups of approximately six participants, about two weeks after the shared educational assessment. A follow-up virtual workshop is offered approximately two weeks later. Finally, optional workshops are also offered according to the participants' needs. The workshop content will be adjusted based on the initial educational assessment to better meet the specific needs of the participants.
4. Finally, 2 to 4 weeks after the end of the educational workshops, a semi-structured individual interview will be conducted by a nurse or physician.

## NCT07786909
ERICA-02 Study : Impact of Physical Exercise on the Tumor Immune Microenvironment in Patients Treated With Neoadjuvant Chemoimmunotherapy for Non-small Cell Lung Cancer

Intervention: : Physical activity recommendations — At the baseline, all participants will receive the international recommendations in terms of physical activity for promoting health in the general population.
: Physical Activity (PA) Intervention — The exercise programme is carried out during the neoadjuvant period (9 to 12 weeks), alongside the three cycles of chemo-immunotherapy. Participants are provided with an exercise bike and a heart rate monitor.

The programme is structured in three progressive phases: adaptation (weeks 1 to 3, light to moderate intensity), progression (weeks 4 to 6, increased intensity and introduction of interval training) and maintenance/optimisation (from week 7 until surgery, stabilisation of the training load).

Outside the treatment weeks, participants undertake one supervised strength training session each week via videoconference and two independent aerobic sessions on an exercise bike. During each treatment session (C1, C2, C3), a supervised session combining aerobic exercise and strength training is carried out whilst the infusion is in progress.A weekly telephone follow-up allows the programme to be adjusted and enables the collection of assessments of fatigue and perceived exertion.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07786909

## NCT07802743
Effects of Blood Flow Restriction Training at Different Limb Occlusion Pressures on Muscle Strength, Lower-Limb Muscle Morphology and Serum Inflammatory Factors in Mechanically Ventilated ICU-Acquired Weakness Patients: A Prospective Three-Arm Randomized Controlled Trial

Intervention: : Blood flow restriction training — Blood flow restriction training (BFRT) is administered using a wireless automated pneumatic cuff system (BFR Cuffs 2.0) placed on the proximal thigh. Training pressure is individually calibrated as a percentage of each patient's resting limb occlusion pressure (LOP), measured via the device's built-in pressure sensor and algorithm to accommodate differences in limb circumference and vascular tone. The intervention partially restricts venous return while preserving arterial inflow, combining low-intensity lower-extremity resistance movements with localized hypoxic and metabolic stress to stimulate muscle anabolic pathways. It is delivered at very low perceived exertion (Borg RPE 9-11) with continuous bedside vital sign monitoring, specifically adapted for hemodynamically stable critically ill patients with ICU-acquired weakness.
: Standard ICU Care and Routine Rehabilitation — This arm serves as the control reference, delivering the standard-of-care clinical pathway for critically ill mechanically ventilated patients. It includes comprehensive ICU medical management (anti-infective treatment, ventilatory support, enteral nutrition, hemodynamic maintenance, electrolyte balance correction, and evidence-based nursing bundles) plus standardized early rehabilitation administered by certified therapists per institutional ICU rehabilitation protocols. No investigational blood flow restriction intervention is applied in this group; all care follows routine clinical practice without additional study-specific exercise modifications.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07802743

SUMMARY:

## NCT07820189
Effects of Microelectrode-based Brain Stimulation in Humans

Intervention: : High Definition Brain Stimulation (HDBS) — The investigational device will be inserted into the brain of awake patients in the same track as planned for the established Deep Brain Stimulation (DBS) electrode. The evaluation will follow a protocol that includes stimulation of predetermined subsets of microelectrodes at up to 4 predetermined levels (depth) in the brain. The stimulation intensity for each subgroup is gradually increased until either a therapeutic effect or an undesirable side effect has been achieved. If an unacceptable effect is elicited, stimulation is immediately interrupted and a new subset of electrodes is tested. After completion of the evaluation, the investigational device will be removed and the implantation of an established permanent DBS electrode is completed.

## NCT07862205
Closed-Loop Electroencephalography Optimized Transcranial Magnetic Stimulation Pilot Study for Advanced Treatment Outcomes in Adults With Major Depressive Disorder

Intervention: : MagVenture TMS Therapy System with Localite Navigation System and actiCHamp EEG — The MagVenture TMS Therapy System is based on the use of intermittent theta burst stimulation, or iTBS. This novel TMS protocol delivers treatment in only 3 minutes and is based on a patterned TMS paradigm delivered to the left-dorsolateral prefrontal cortex (LDPFC) at 120% of resting motor threshold (MT). It consists of bursts of 3 pulses at 50 Hz, with bursts repeated at 5 Hz, in a duty cycle of 2s on, 8 s off, over 3 minutes and 9 seconds, and a total of 600 pulses.

For peak or trough iTBS, stimulation will consist of 3 pulses at 50 Hz delivered at intervals of their individual theta rhythm (e.g. 200 msec for a 5Hz oscillation) followed by an 8 second rest period. The pulses will be aligned to either the peak or the trough of the theta rhythm at the beginning of each pulse train. The TMS therapy will be delivered to the Left DLPFC at 120% of resting motor threshold (MT) daily for 5 days per week, for 6 weeks.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07862205

## NCT07910045
Effects of Scapulothoracic Stabilization Exercises on Clinical Outcomes and Occupational Performance in Violinists: A Randomized Controlled Trial

Intervention: : Postural Correction and Flexibility Exercise Program — Participants will perform a home-based exercise program consisting of postural correction and flexibility exercises for 6 weeks, three days per week, with each session lasting approximately 30-45 minutes. The program will include isometric scapular retraction exercises and chin tuck exercises, each performed for 5 seconds and 10 repetitions. Stretching exercises targeting the upper trapezius, levator scapulae, and pectoralis minor muscles, as well as posterior capsule stretching, will be performed for 20-30 seconds and 3 repetitions. Pendulum exercises will be performed for 1-2 minutes, and shoulder circumduction exercises will be performed for 10 repetitions.
: Scapulothoracic Stabilization Exercise Program — Participants will perform the same postural correction and flexibility exercise program as the control group, with the addition of scapulothoracic stabilization strengthening exercises. The exercises will target the serratus anterior, upper, middle, and lower trapezius, rhomboid, and latissimus dorsi muscles. The strengthening exercises will be performed using resistance bands, with 3 sets of 10 repetitions. Exercise intensity will be adjusted according to the participant's perceived level of difficulty. The intervention will be performed for 6 weeks, three days per week, with each session lasting approximately 30-45 minutes, under the supervision of a physiotherapist.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07910045

SUMMARY:

## NCT07910916
Effects of Concurrent Training on Serum PCSK9 Levels in Adults With Overweight: A Randomized Controlled Trial

Intervention: : Concurrent Aerobic and Resistance Training — Participants in the intervention group will complete an eight-week supervised concurrent aerobic and resistance training program, consisting of three sessions per week. Each 60-minute session will include a 5-minute warm-up, 25 minutes of aerobic exercise, 25 minutes of resistance exercise, and a 5-minute cool-down.

Aerobic exercise will be performed on a treadmill or cycle ergometer at 40-60% of heart rate reserve.

Resistance training will include eight exercises targeting major muscle groups: chest press, lat pull-down, hamstring curl, quadriceps extension, shoulder press, biceps curl, triceps extension, and abdominal crunch. Participants will perform two sets of 8-16 repetitions at 50-80% of their 10-repetition maximum, with approximately one minute of rest between sets.

All sessions will be conducted under professional supervision. Heart rate and perceived exertion will be monitored during exercise using a heart rate monitor and the Borg Rating of Perceived Exertion Scale.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07910916

SUMMARY:

## NCT07911631
Exploring the Impact of Self-Compassion on Psychological Adjustment and Its Mechanisms Among Breast Cancer Survivors

Intervention: : Mindful Self-Compassion — An adapted eight-week Mindful Self-Compassion (MSC) group program for breast cancer survivors, delivered online through live videoconferencing in weekly 105-minute sessions. A licensed clinical psychologist with MSC-related training and group facilitation experience will lead the program, supported by a research staff member with clinical psychology training. The program retains core MSC content involving mindfulness, self-kindness, and common humanity. Activities include guided mindfulness and self-compassion practices, compassionate writing, group sharing, and exercises for responding to difficult emotions and relationships. Participants will receive home-practice guidance and be invited to record practice frequency, duration, and type. To reduce participation demands, sessions are shortened from the standard 150 minutes to 105 minutes, and the midprogram four-hour retreat is omitted.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07911631

SUMMARY:

## NCT07912372
Comprehensive Assessment of Rehabilitation Experience Using the Social And Family Evaluation (SAFE) Scale

Intervention: : Caregiver training and administration of SAFE scale — The intervention will consist of caregiver training and completion of the SAFE scale (Magnani et al., 2025). The SAFE is an observational tool developed to allow caregivers of patients with Disorders of Consciousness (DoC) to systematically report cognitively mediated behaviours based on daily interactions with the patient over the previous two weeks. Derived from the Coma Recovery Scale-Revised (CRS-R; Giacino et al., 2020), the SAFE includes simplified items designed for non-professional users. Preliminary results have shown good reliability, internal consistency, and correlation with the CRS-R. Following a standardized explanation provided by trained clinicians, caregivers will complete the scale by reporting behaviours observed during the previous 15 days. For each item, caregivers will answer "Yes" or "No" regarding the presence of a specific voluntary/non-reflexive behaviour associated with sensory or cognitive domains.
: Standard clinical care — Caregivers allocated to the NO-SAFE group will receive the standard clinical care provided by the rehabilitation unit. They will receive the Fondazione Don Gnocchi educational booklet "Il paziente con GCA: guida per le famiglie" as the minimum psychoeducational intervention and will participate in routine clinical meetings with the physicians of the rehabilitation unit.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07912372

SUMMARY:

## NCT07913347
Phase 1b/2a Study of MGT-006 in Patients With Ulcerative Colitis: A Dose and Preparation Regimen Selection Trial to Assess Safety and Microbiome Engraftment (ROOT Study)

Intervention: : MGT-006 — MGT-006 is an investigational enteric-coated oral capsule filled with lyophilized human fecal material containing viable gut microbiota. In Phase 1b, four capsules are taken three times daily before meals for 56 days (12 capsules per day). Dose reduction, interruption, discontinuation, and re-escalation may occur according to protocol-defined safety rules. Phase 2a doses, dosing frequency, and treatment duration will be determined based on Phase 1b findings and specified in an amended protocol.
: Vancomycin hydrochloride — Vancomycin hydrochloride is administered orally as pretreatment at a total daily dose of 500 mg (as vancomycin), divided into four daily administrations, for five days before MGT-006. In Phase 1b, it is administered only to participants randomized to the vancomycin pretreatment arm. Its use in Phase 2a depends on the pretreatment approach selected from Phase 1b.
: Amoxicillin hydrate/ Fosfomycin calcium hydrate/ Metronidazole — Subjects allocated to the AFM group will receive pretreatment antibiotics for 5 days after final registration. Amoxicillin hydrate 1500 mg/day (as amoxicillin), fosfomycin calcium hydrate 3000 mg/day (as fosfomycin), and metronidazole 750 mg/day will be orally administered three times a day for 5 days. Its use in Phase 2a depends on the pretreatment approach selected from Phase 1b.

## NCT07913620
"Experimental Study on Stroke Rehabilitation Based on Dual-Task Exercises"

Intervention: : Dual-Task Perturbation & Computerized Cognitive Training — The motor component consists of structured treadmill- or platform-based postural perturbational challenges designed to evoke reactive balance responses. Concurrently, participants will perform computerized cognitive exercises displayed on an eye-level screen targeting four core cognitive domains: memory, attention, language, and reasoning.

To ensure both standardization across subjects and individualized progression, participants will execute a fixed set of five validated NeuronUP tasks during every session:

Forming Sentences. Interview. Traffic Fines. Bottle Caps. Pass the Ball

## NCT07914218
Remotely Improving Physical Activity in Rural Veterans With Cognitive Impairment and Care Partner Dyads

Intervention: : Telerehabilitation Physical Activity Behavioral Intervention — Six dyads (n=12) will complete TPAB field testing and will undergo testing at baseline and post-intervention. Consent, baseline testing, intervention delivery, and post-testing will be conducted virtually via Microsoft Teams and data recorded in VA REDCap. Testing sessions will not be recorded, with the exception of semi-structured interviews during post-testing. The testing procedures will take approximately 2 hours to complete at each testing time point. The TPAB intervention will involve weekly 30-minute sessions with study personnel for 6 weeks. Due to the 7-day activity monitor data collection prior to and following the 6-week TPAB intervention, participants will be enrolled in the study for approximately 8 weeks. Three dyads will be asked to join the advisory board with the research team to assist with implementing the walking program adaptations based on the collective feedback across the 16 dyads.

## NCT07914270
Optimization of Aquatic Power Training Dosing Strategies and the Effects of Creatine Supplementation on Functional and Multisystem Adaptations in Older Adults: A Randomized Controlled Trial

Intervention: : RPE-1 Aquatic Power Training-Placebo — Participants will complete a 16-week supervised aquatic power training program, consisting of three sessions per week, with each session lasting approximately 60 minutes. Exercises will be performed at maximal intended movement velocity.

For each exercise, participants will perform 4 sets of 8 repetitions. Exercise intensity will be regulated using ratings of perceived exertion (RPE) obtained during the set. During the first repetition (RPE-1), the target RPE will be 4 for the active muscles or 3 for the overall body. By the last repetition of the set, the target RPE will be 6 for the active muscles or 5 for the overall body.

Participants will perform active recovery for 120 seconds between sets and 90 seconds between exercises.

This RPE-based dosing strategy will be used to individualize the aquatic power training stimulus according to each participant's perceived exertion while maintaining maximal intended movement velocity.
: Time-Based Aquatic Power Training-Placebo — Participants will complete a 16-week supervised aquatic power training program, consisting of three sessions per week, with each session lasting approximately 60 minutes. Exercises will be performed at maximal intended movement velocity.

The training structure will follow the same general protocol as the RPE-1 aquatic power training condition. However, instead of prescribing exercise volume according to a fixed number of repetitions regulated by RPE-1, participants will perform each exercise for an execution time equivalent to that required to complete the RPE-1 protocol.

Participants will be instructed to perform the exercises at maximal intended movement velocity throughout the prescribed execution period. The maximu

Participants will receive a placebo visually indistinguishable from the creatine supplement. The placebo will be administered daily throughout the 16-week intervention, including non-training days, following the same supplementation schedule as the creatine condition.
: Time-Based Aquatic Power Training + Creatine — Participants will complete a 16-week supervised aquatic power training program, consisting of three sessions per week, with each session lasting approximately 60 minutes. Exercises will be performed at maximal intended movement velocity.

The training structure will follow the same general protocol as the RPE-1 aquatic power training condition. However, instead of prescribing exercise volume according to a fixed number of repetitions regulated by RPE-1, participants will perform each exercise for an execution time equivalent to that required to complete the RPE-1 protocol.

Participants will be instructed to perform the exercises at maximal intended movement velocity throughout the prescribed execution period. The maxi

Participants will additionally receive 3 g/day of creatine monohydrate throughout the 16-week intervention. Participants will receive 3 g/day of creatine monohydrate for 16 weeks. Creatine will be administered daily, including non-training days, without a loading phase.
: Sedentary Control + Placebo — Participants allocated to this group will not participate in the aquatic power training intervention and will receive placebo supplementation daily throughout the 16-week study period. Participants will be instructed to maintain their usual lifestyle and habitual physical activity and not to initiate a structured moderate- or high-intensity strength or power training program during the intervention period. Participants will receive a placebo visually indistinguishable from the creatine supplement. The placebo will be administered daily throughout the 16-week intervention following the same supplementation schedule as the creatine condition.
: Sedentary Control + Creatine — Participants allocated to this group will not participate in the aquatic power training intervention and will receive 3 g/day of creatine monohydrate throughout the 16-week study period. Participants will be instructed to maintain their usual lifestyle and habitual physical activity and not to initiate a structured moderate- or high-intensity strength or power training program during the intervention period.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07914270

## NCT07914634
Exploring the Feasibility of Virtual Reality-Assisted Trauma-Focused Cognitive Behavioural Therapy for PTSD: A Mixed Methods Study

Intervention: : VR Photoscan — VR Photoscan is an immersive virtual reality intervention that uses digitally reconstructed three-dimensional environments representing trauma-related locations. The technology enables patients to revisit and engage with these environments within a controlled therapeutic setting, supporting trauma-focused exposure, memory processing and the reduction of avoidance behaviours. VR Photoscan is delivered as an adjunct to trauma-focused cognitive behavioural therapy (TF-CBT), under the guidance and supervision of a qualified clinician within a specialist psychological therapies service.
: Trauma-Focused Cognitive Behavioural Therapy (TF-CBT) — Trauma-focused cognitive behavioural therapy (TF-CBT) is an evidence-based psychological intervention for the treatment of post-traumatic stress disorder (PTSD). The therapy aims to reduce PTSD symptoms through the processing of traumatic memories, cognitive restructuring, and addressing trauma-related avoidance behaviours. Treatment is delivered by qualified clinicians within a specialist psychological therapies service in Northern Ireland, following established clinical protocols and individualised treatment plans. The frequency and duration of therapy are determined by individual clinical needs.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07914634

## NCT06350929
Evaluation of Norepinephrine in the Management of General Anesthesia-Induced Arterial Hypotension in Chronic Heart Failure Patients in the Operating Room.

Intervention: : Cardiac output monitoring by the Starling™ SV Sebac® — During the surgery, the channels and monitoring equipment will be set up according to the usual procedure in the vascular surgery department. As part of the research, the Starling™ SV Sebac® electrodes will be installed (to measure cardiac flow, cardiac index (CI), stroke volume (VES), variations in stroke volume (VVES), and peripheral resistors (TPRI)).

Once the equipment is in place, anesthetic induction will be carried out according to the usual protocol with curarization and mechanical ventilation.

If the appearance of arterial hypotension is noted after anesthetic induction and mechanical ventilation, the patient will be definitively included in the study. As recommended, he will receive norepinephrine to treat hypotension.

The measurements from the monitoring will be collected at 3, 5, and 10 min after the administration of norepinephrine.

10 days after admission to the operating room, hospitalization data will be collected.

## NCT06646991
Comparison of Several Oxygenation Targets With Different Oxygen Interfaces - Impact on Oxygen Flow Rates

Intervention: : Nasal canula - up to 6 liters per minute — Oxygen will be administered via nasal cannula up to 6 liters per minute during this period.

Oxygen flow rates will be adjusted manually, with a gradual increase in flow rates to achieve a oxygen flow rates for reach the peripheral oxygen saturation (SpO2) target (92% for non chronic obstructive pulmonary disease (non COPD) patient and 90% for chronic obstructive pulmonary disease (COPD) patient).

The steady state will be defined by a fixed oxygen flow rate and a stable SpO2 around the target ±2% for 15 minutes. The average oxygen flow rate during the last five minutes will be used.
: Nasal canula - up to 15 liters per minute — Oxygen will be administered via nasal cannula up to 15 liters per minute during this period.

Oxygen flow rates will be adjusted manually, with a gradual increase in flow rates to achieve a oxygen flow rates for reach the SpO2 target (92% for non COPD patient and 90% for COPD patient).

The steady state will be defined by a fixed oxygen flow rate and a stable SpO2 around the target ±2% for 15 minutes. The average oxygen flow rate during the last five minutes will be used.
: Open oxygen mask — Oxygen will be administered via an open during this period. Oxygen flow rates will be adjusted manually, with a gradual increase in flow rates to achieve a oxygen flow rates for reach the SpO2 target (92% for non COPD patient and 90% for COPD patient).

The steady state will be defined by a fixed oxygen flow rate and a stable SpO2 around the target ±2% for 15 minutes. The average oxygen flow rate during the last five minutes will be used.
: Simple oxygen face mask — Oxygen will be administered via simple oxygen face mask during this period. Oxygen flow rates will be adjusted manually, with a gradual increase in flow rates to achieve a oxygen flow rates for reach the SpO2 target (92% for non COPD patient and 90% for COPD patient).

The steady state will be defined by a fixed oxygen flow rate and a stable SpO2 around the target ±2% for 15 minutes. The average oxygen flow rate during the last five minutes will be used.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT06646991

## NCT07346911

Intervention: : Transdiagnostic app intervention — A transdiagnostic, self-guided CBT app, called TankeVirus (literally translated Thought Viruses), consisting of three components:

1. Standardized self-assessment for mental health: 'Test your psychological immune system'.
2. Educational mini-series: A 30-minute, entertaining video series that teaches users to build resilience by addressing essential needs like sleep (psychological immune system), recognizing negative thought patterns (thought viruses), and developing healthier coping mechanisms (psychological vitamins).
3. Daily practice: Over 20 days, users receive a short, 1-2-minute video each morning. These videos provide practical tips, small experiments, or simple tasks designed to help users integrate healthier coping strategies into their daily lives.

The intervention was adapted from an empirically-supported mindfulness-based intervention for adolescents (Learning to BREATHE; Broderick, 2021). Example activities include breath awareness, body scanning, mindful eating, loving-kindness practice, and mindful movement, as well as activities and discussion focused on improving distress tolerance and enhancing self-care, with examples related to living with migraine.
: Enhanced Standard-of-Care: Headache Education — Enhanced Standard-of-Care is a 6-session group headache education program delivered via telehealth. It will be derived from a manual with fidelity as an attention-matched control in a behavioral trial for pediatric migraine. Headache education will include didactics and discussion on headache symptoms, medication, lifestyle factors for pain management, communication with providers, education on mood/stress, and impact of headaches on school and family.

## NCT07577310
False Vocal Fold vs EMG-Guided Botulinum Toxin Injections for Voice Tremor (FVFIVT): Prospective Non-Randomized Comparative Clinical Trial

Intervention: : False Vocal Fold (FVF) botulinum toxin injections — False vocal fold (FVF) injection, or supraglottic injection, is a specialized treatment primarily using Botox to treat severe Adductor Spasmodic Dysphonia (ADSD). It involves injecting botulinum toxin into the false cords to reduce involuntary squeezing (hyperadduction), offering a potential alternative or supplement to traditional true vocal fold injections to minimize breathiness while improving voice quality.
: Electromyography (EMG)-Guided Injection — EMG-guided injection is a precise medical technique using electromyography (EMG) to locate specific muscles for botulinum toxin (Botox/Dysport) injections. It uses auditory and visual signals to confirm needle placement in active muscles, enhancing accuracy for treating spasticity, dystonia, and laryngological conditions.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07577310

## NCT07626658
Food_i Sense Analytics: Integrando la Inteligencia Artificial Con la monitorización Continua de la Glucosa Para la nutrición de precisión

Intervention: : Continuous glucose monitoring using a wearable sensor (flash interstitial glucose monitor) — The intervention consists of applying and wearing a 14-day continuous glucose monitoring (CGM) device that captures interstitial glucose every minute under free-living conditions. This wearable flash sensor is used exclusively for passive data collection; it does not provide insulin delivery, therapeutic adjustments, or real-time clinical management. What distinguishes this intervention is its integration into a multimodal data-capture system: participants simultaneously complete structured dietary records, submit standardized meal photographs for AI-based food recognition, and undergo detailed phenotyping. The CGM data are then processed through the study's proprietary GLIA algorithm to derive individualized glucose-response patterns ("glucotypes"). This combination of high-frequency glucose monitoring, dietary image analytics, and machine-learning modeling differentiates the device's use from typical clinical or self-management applications in other studies.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07626658

SUMMARY:

## NCT07684742
Early Single Antiplatelet Therapy With a Potent P2Y12 Inhibitor After Intravascular Ultrasound-Guided PCI in Patients With Acute Coronary Syndrome: A Multicenter Randomized Controlled Trial

Intervention: : Early P2Y12 Inhibitor Monotherapy — Participants randomized within 96 hours after successful IVUS-guided PCI will discontinue aspirin immediately after randomization and receive potent P2Y12 inhibitor monotherapy with ticagrelor 90 mg twice daily or prasugrel 10 mg once daily. However, in patients aged ≥75 years, weighing \<60 kg, or considered at high bleeding risk, a reduced dose of ticagrelor (60 mg twice daily) or prasugrel (5 mg once daily) may be administered at the operator's discretion.
: Standard Dual Antiplatelet Therapy — Participants randomized within 96 hours after successful IVUS-guided PCI will receive aspirin 100 mg once daily plus a potent P2Y12 inhibitor, consisting of ticagrelor 90 mg twice daily or prasugrel 10 mg once daily, for 12 months. However, in patients aged ≥75 years, weighing \<60 kg, or considered at high bleeding risk, a reduced dose of ticagrelor (60 mg twice daily) or prasugrel (5 mg once daily) may be administered at the operator's discretion.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07684742

## NCT07685912
Enhancing Caregiver Wellbeing: The Role of Exercise, Nutrition, and Stress Management for Caregivers of Patients With Advanced Cardiac and Pulmonary Disease

Intervention: : Multimodal Well-being Program — The multimodal caregiver wellness Program includes three core components: flexible physical activity or exercise program tailored to the goals of caregivers, nutritional counselling, and stress management strategies delivered through the exercise professional and registered dietician. Participants will be encouraged to engage in aerobic exercise, with a goal of gradually increasing activity levels toward guideline-based targets, complemented by resistance training or other preferred forms of physical activity. The nutritional component includes one individualized 45-60-minute counselling session with a registered dietitian, based on a brief nutrition screening tool tailored to the caregiver's health needs. Participants may request follow-up support throughout the intervention period. Stress management is embedded based on participant availability, interactions with the exercise professional, and focusing on integrating simple stress-reduction strategies into daily routines.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07685912

## NCT07726628
Video-assisted TELEmedecine for Medical Dispatch of Adult Patients Calling to an Emergency Medical Communication Centre for Acute DYSpnea: a Multicentre Stepped-wedge Cluster Randomised Controlled Study

Intervention: : Addition of telemedine to standard medical dispatching process — Patients included during the intervention phase will undergo video-assisted medical triage.

Video-assisted triage will be performed using a secure web-based telemedicine hosted on a certified health data server. The emergency physician responsible for medical regulation will send a secure web link via SMS to the caller's smartphone or, when appropriate, to the smartphone of a relative or bystander. Once the link has been activated, a live video stream captured by the smartphone camera will be securely transmitted in real time to the physician's workstation at the Emergency Medical Communication Center, enabling direct visualization of the patient's clinical condition during the remote assessment.

## NCT07859644
Effect of a Mobile Nutrition Application on Dietary Behaviour Among Adults in a Low- and Middle-Income Country: A Randomized Controlled Trial

Intervention: : Nutritional mobile phone application — Kalorist is a nutrition-coaching platform built for South Asian population (desis) all over the world, delivering the kind of personalized, evidence-based support a client gets working one-on-one with a nutritionist, via a mobile app. It follows an assess-guide-monitor-adjust cycle: participant profile data (age, height, weight, gender) and goals set individualized calorie and macronutrient (carbohydrate, protein, fat) targets; participants log food by description, photo, or search, including local dishes and restaurants. Kal, an AI coach built inside, answers users' questions about their own patterns - why weight has plateaued or spiked, why cravings appear after skipping meals - using research-backed explanations rather than opinions, gives behavior- and habit-based guidance, and offers motivational support on request. Meal plans adapt to actual eating. KalLive gives an instant photo-based calorie/macro breakdown to save or share.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07859644

## NCT07874217
Whole-body Cryotherapy in Those With Chronic Fatigue: A Feasibility Study

Intervention: : Cryotherapy — Intervention: Whole-body cryotherapy intervention consists of 10 sessions administered over 2 consecutive weeks. Sessions will be conducted in an indirect liquid nitrogen-cooled cryochamber with a prechamber set at -60∘°C and a main chamber at -120∘°C. To maximise safety and tolerability in this sensitive patient population, the protocol features a gradual increase in exposure time. Following a 30-second familiarisation session (sessions 1 \& 2), exposure time in the main chamber will increase incrementally from 1 minute on day 1 to a maximum of 3.5 minutes on day 10. Participants will wear standardised protective clothing (gloves, socks, headband, and mask) during all sessions.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07874217

SUMMARY:

## NCT07894484
Lactium Premedication to Prevent Emergence Agitation: A Dose-Finding Study in Children Undergoing Adenotonsillectomy

Intervention: : Lactium — Lactium solution will be prepared by dissolving each one lentra sachet (150 mg lactium per sachet) in 50 ml of water so that each ml will contain 3 mg of lactium. Oral lactium will be initiated at a dose of 4 mg/kg in the first patient. Dose in subsequent cases will be determined by response to lactium in previous patient using absence or presence of emergence agitation as our primary outcome. A dosing interval of (1 mg/kg) will be used. If the child has no agitation, the subsequent one will receive the same dose with a probability of 0.89 or a reduced dose by 1 mg/kg with a probability of 1/9 (0.11). If the child develops agitation, the subsequent dose will be increased by 1 mg/kg with 100% certainty. If the child responds to the dose of 4 mg/kg, the dose for the next child will remain the same, since this is our minimum dose.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07894484

SUMMARY:

## NCT07895602
The Effect of Custom 3D Printed Insoles and Short Foot Exercises on Foot Posture, Balance, and Gait Parameters in Individuals With Pes Planus

Intervention: : Custom 3D Printed Insoles and Short Foot Exercises — The intervention consists of a combined biomechanical and active rehabilitation approach for individuals with pes planus:

1. Custom 3D Printed Insoles: The participants' feet will be 3D scanned, and custom insoles will be manufactured using 3D printing technology. Participants will be recommended to use these personalized insoles during their daily activities for a total of 8 weeks.
2. Short Foot Exercises (SFE): Participants will perform a short foot exercise (foot core / intrinsic muscle) program aimed at increasing intrinsic foot muscle activation. The exercise protocol will be performed daily for 8 weeks, with each repetition held for five seconds.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07895602

## NCT07895719
Exercise and Diet Supplements for Older Patients Undergoing TAVI: the IRON-TAVI Trial

Intervention: : Personalized Multidomain Exercise Program — Participants assigned to the multidomain intervention will receive a personalized exercise training program beginning before transcatheter aortic valve implantation and continuing for three months after the procedure. The program includes five supervised exercise sessions, two before and three after the procedure, together with a structured home-based exercise program. Exercise includes walking, mobility exercises, sit-to-stand exercises, balance training, and progressive functional strengthening. Exercise intensity and progression will be individualized according to symptoms, physical performance, clinical condition, and exercise tolerance.
: Oral Protein and Iron Nutritional Supplementation — Participants assigned to the multidomain intervention will receive individualized nutritional counseling and daily oral nutritional supplementation containing protein and iron. Supplementation will start after randomization, before transcatheter aortic valve implantation, and will continue for three months after the procedure. The nutritional intervention will be adapted according to baseline nutritional status, body weight, appetite, renal function, clinical condition, and tolerance. Adherence and tolerability will be monitored throughout the intervention period.
: Usual Care — Participants assigned to the control group will receive standard care according to current clinical practice, including transcatheter aortic valve implantation, guideline-directed medical management, routine follow-up, and general advice regarding physical activity and a balanced diet. No structured study-driven exercise program or protocol-mandated nutritional supplementation will be provided.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07895719

SUMMARY:

A Single-Arm, Prospective, Phase II Clinical Study of Zemetostat Combined With Anlotinib in Patients With Recurrent/Metastatic Head and Neck Soft Tissue Sarcoma With SWI/SNF Complex Deficiency After Failure of Standard-of-Care Therapy

Intervention: : zemetostat combined with anlotinib — Enrolled patients receive zemetostat plus anlotinib until discontinuation criteria are met. The specific dosing regimen is as follows: zemetostat 350 mg orally twice daily (bid po). The recommended dose of anlotinib is 12 mg once daily for 14 consecutive days, followed by a 7-day drug-free interval (2/1 schedule), constituting a 21-day treatment cycle. Doses should be taken at approximately the same time each day. If a patient vomits or misses a dose, no supplemental dose should be administered on that day; the subsequent scheduled dose should be taken as usual.

## NCT07918222
Sequential Treatment Approaches for Treatment-Resistant Schizophrenia (STATRS): A Randomized Controlled Trial of Pharmacological Optimization and Neuromodulation in Chinese Patients

Intervention: : [Phase 1] Clozapine — Initial dose: 25-50 mg/day orally. Clozapine will be individually titrated over 8 weeks, with a usual target dose of 300-450 mg/day and a target steady-state trough plasma concentration of ≥350 ng/mL. Plasma clozapine concentration will be assessed at Week 4.

If the concentration is \<350 ng/mL, the dose will be further increased as clinically tolerated. A dose of ≥300 mg/day is not required if the target plasma concentration is achieved at a lower dose. Participants who cannot reach either 300 mg/day or the target plasma concentration because of intolerance may remain on their maximum tolerated dose below 300 mg/day.
: [Phase 1] Aripiprazole — Initial dose: 5-10 mg/day. Aripiprazole recommended dosage:10-15 mg/day. dosage form:po.duration:8 weeks
: [Phase 1] Amisulpride — Initial dose: 200-400 mg/day. Amisulpride recommended dosage: 400-800 mg/day. dosage form:po.duration:8 weeks
: [Phase 2] Modified Electroconvulsive Therapy (MECT) — MECT will be administered using a Thymatron System IV device. The electrical charge will be determined according to the participant's age and body weight, with treatment parameters adjusted according to the study protocol and clinical response. Participants will continue the pharmacotherapy regimen received before Phase 2. Medication doses will remain stable whenever clinically feasible; changes will be permitted only when clinically necessary for safety or tolerability and will be documented.
: [Phase 2] Magnetic Seizure Therapy (MST) — MST will be administered using a MagPro X100 magnetic stimulator equipped with the MagOption MST module. The stimulation frequency will be set at 100 Hz and output intensity at 100% of device output. Stimulation duration will be titrated according to the study protocol and individual participant response. Participants will continue the pharmacotherapy regimen received before Phase 2. Medication doses will remain stable whenever clinically feasible; changes will be permitted only when clinically necessary for safety or tolerability and will be documented.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07918222

SUMMARY:

Predictive Value of dPmax Measured by Pulse Contour Analysis for Postoperative Right Ventricular Failure in Patients Undergoing LVAD Implantation

Intervention: : Arterial Pulse Contour Analysis — Arterial pulse contour analysis will be performed using the PulsioFlex monitoring system during routine invasive arterial monitoring for LVAD implantation. dPmax, representing the maximum rate of rise in arterial pressure, will be recorded before and after anesthesia induction. ## NCT07918872
Effect of Squatting Position on Birth Outcomes in the Second Stage of Labor in Nulliparous Women: A Randomized Controlled Trial/ Nullipar Kadınlarda Doğumun İkinci Evresinde Çömelme Pozisyonu Uygulamasının Doğum Sonuçlarına Etkisi: Randomize Kontrollü Çalışma

Intervention: : Experimental (Squatting Position) — Until the second stage of labor (until cervical dilation reaches 10 cm), routine clinical care will continue to be provided.

During the second stage of labor, in the active phase when the pregnant woman begins to feel spontaneous urges to push, she will be supported to assume a squatting position on the birthing bed. The woman will adopt the squatting position during uterine contractions and will be allowed to rest in any position she desires on the bed between contractions.

Delivery Phase: After the fetal head crowns, the upper section (head section) of the birthing bed will be elevated to an angle between 45 and 60 degrees. The pregnant woman will lean against the backrest, position her buttocks on the seating area, and place her legs in flexion at the knees, pressing her feet onto the edges of the bed. While the pregnant woman is held in this position, the delivery of the fetus will be performed by Meryem Özdemir, a specialist midwife who is part of the research team.
: Routin Care (Lithotomy Position) — Nulliparous pregnant women included in this control group will receive routine clinical care, and no experimental intervention will be performed until the crowning stage (when the fetal scalp becomes visible at the perineum). For this group, following standard intrapartum protocols, the upper section (head section) of the birthing bed is elevated to an angle of less than 45 degrees during delivery. The pregnant woman is placed in the conventional lithotomy position by positioning her legs on the foot supports attached to the right and left sides of the birthing bed. The deliveries of the pregnant women included in this control group will be conducted in the lithotomy position by the researcher, Expert Midwife Meryem Ozdemir.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07918872

SUMMARY:

## NCT07919392
Restoring Emotional Needs With Exogenous Ketones for Women

Intervention: : Exogenous ketone supplementation — Participants will consume Qitone exogenous ketone supplement orally twice daily for 16 weeks. Supplements will be pre-packaged and labeled with identification codes to maintain blinding. Adherence will be monitored through self-report logs and returned packaging counts.
: Matched placebo beverage — Participants will consume an inert placebo beverage orally twice daily for 16 weeks. The placebo will be matched to the Qitone supplement in appearance and taste. It will be pre-packaged and labeled with identification codes to maintain blinding. Adherence will be monitored through self-report logs and returned packaging counts.
: Group-Mediated Cognitive Behavioral Counseling — Participants will attend one 30-45-minute virtual Group-Mediated Cognitive Behavioral counseling session per week for 16 weeks. Sessions will be led by trained behavioral specialists and emphasize group support, cognitive reframing, self-regulation, motivation, and adherence to exercise and nutrition recommendations. Content will progress through intensive, transition, and maintenance phases.
: Structured Group Exercise — Participants will attend two 30-minute virtual group exercise sessions per week for 16 weeks. Sessions will combine resistance and interval-based training led by certified instructors. Exercise intensity and progression will be individualized based on perceived exertion and postpartum safety. Participants will also be encouraged to achieve at least 150 minutes of moderate-to-vigorous physical activity per week.

## NCT07919444
Effectiveness of Phenotype-based Pain Management Guidelines for Head and Neck Cancer Related Pain: a Pilot Study

Intervention: : myofascial pain — Participants with myofascial pain disorder phenotype will receive a muscle relaxant, such as tizanidine (up to 8 mg a day), cyclobenzaprine (up to 30 mg a day) or amitriptyline (up to 25 mg a day). In addition, depending on the pain intensity, they will receive non-opioid analgesics or opioids. The participants with pain intensity of less than 7 out of possible 10, on a NVRS, will receive non-opioid analgesics (acetaminophen and non-steroidal anti-inflammatory medications). However, if the intensity of pain is higher (7 or more), they will receive non-opioid analgesics and opioids (codeine, tramadol or morphine). For pain with intensity between 7 and 8 out of possible 10, on a NRVS, tramadol (up to 400 mg a day) or codeine (up to 90 mg a day) will be used. However, if the intensity of pain is 9 or more of 10, morphine (up to 60 mg a day) will be used.
: Orofacial bone pain — The participants with a phenotype of orofacial bone pain and a pain intensity of less than 7 out of a possible 10 on an NVRS will receive non-opioid analgesics (acetaminophen and non-steroidal anti-inflammatory medications). However, if the intensity of pain is higher (7 or more), they will receive non-opioid analgesics and opioids (codeine, tramadol or morphine). For pain with an intensity between 7 and 8 out of a possible 10 on an NRVS, tramadol (up to 400 mg a day) or codeine (up to 90 mg a day) will be used. However, if the intensity of pain is 9 or more of 10, morphine (up to 60 mg a day) will be used.
: Orofacial Mucosal Pain — The participants with a phenotype of orofacial mucosal pain and a pain intensity of less than 7 out of a possible 10 on an NVRS will receive non-opioid analgesics (acetaminophen and non-steroidal anti-inflammatory medications). However, if the intensity of pain is higher (7 or more), they will receive non-opioid analgesics and opioids (codeine, tramadol, or morphine). For pain with intensity between 7 and 8 out of possible 10, on an NRVS, tramadol (up to 400 mg a day) or codeine (up to 90 mg a day) will be used. However, if the intensity of pain is 9 or more of 10, morphine (up to 60 mg a day) will be used.
: Neuralgia Pain — Those with neuralgia pain phenotype will receive pregabalin (up to 600 mg a day) or carbamazepine (up to 600 mg a day).
: Burning Pain — The participants with burning disorder phenotype will receive pregabalin (up to 600 mg a day) or clonazepam (up to 2 mg a day).
: Persistent Pain Phenotype — The participants with persistent pain disorder phenotype will receive amitriptyline (up to 25 mg a day) or pregabalin (up to 600 mg a day).
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07919444

SUMMARY:

## NCT07919457
Phenotype-based Model for Managing Chronic Post-treatment Pain Among Head and Neck Cancer Survivors: a Pilot Study

Intervention: : Myofascial Pain — Participants with myofascial pain disorder will initially receive muscle relaxants, such as tizanidine (up to 8 mg per day), cyclobenzaprine (up to 30 mg per day), or amitriptyline (up to 25 mg per day). When the reported pain intensity is 7 or more out of 10, these participants will additionally undergo masticatory or cervical trigger point injections.
: Orofacial Bone Pain — Participants identified with orofacial bone pain disorder who report a pain intensity of less than 7 out of a possible 10 on the Numeric Verbal Rating Scale (NVRS) will receive non-opioid analgesics, including acetaminophen and non-steroidal anti-inflammatory medications. In cases where the pain intensity is 7 or more, participants will be offered targeted nerve blocks. These blocks will be performed along the sensory nerves supplying the affected region to provide localized pain relief.
: Neuralgia Pain — Participants classified with neuralgia will be treated with pregabalin (up to 600 mg per day) or carbamazepine (up to 1200 mg per day). Nerve blocks will be considered for those experiencing pain of 7 or more out of 10. In summary, participants with persistent pain disorder, burning disorder, or neuralgia phenotypes who present with pain intensity scores of 7 or higher will be given nerve blocks around the sensory nerves corresponding to the region of pain.
: Burning Pain — Participants with burning pain will receive pregabalin (up to 600 mg per day), carbamazepine (up to 1200 mg per day), or clonazepam (up to 2 mg per day), and if their pain intensity meets or exceeds 7 out of 10, nerve blocks will similarly be provided to target the sensory pathways involved.
: Persistent Pain — Those diagnosed with persistent pain disorder will be treated with either amitriptyline (up to 25 mg per day) or pregabalin (up to 600 mg per day). Participants in this group who report pain intensity of 7 or more will also be offered nerve blocks administered at sites corresponding to the involved sensory distribution.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07919457

SUMMARY:

## NCT07919587
Effect of Repetitive Transcranial Magnetic Stimulation Versus Theta Burst Stimulation on Impulsivity, Emotional Dysregulation, and Non-Suicidal Self-Injury in a Sample of Patients With Borderline Personality Disorder: A Randomized Controlled Trial

Intervention: : Repetitive Transcranial Magnetic Stimulation — rTMS will be delivered at 5 Hz frequency, 1200 pulses per session, at 80-110% of the individual's resting motor threshold (RMT). Stimulation will be applied to the left DLPFC (F3 according to the 10-20 EEG system) using a figure-of-eight coil. Each session includes 18 minutes of active stimulation followed by 3 minutes of sham stimulation on the target site. Sessions are administered 5 days per week for 4 weeks (total 20 sessions).
: Intermittent Theta-Burst Stimulation — iTBS will be delivered as 50 Hz bursts (three pulses per burst, 5 Hz bursts) at 80-120% of resting motor threshold, totaling 600 pulses per session. Stimulation will be applied over the left DLPFC (F3 site, 10-20 EEG system). Each session includes 3 minutes of active iTBS and 18 minutes of sham stimulation. Sessions are administered 5 days per week for 4 weeks (total 20 sessions).
: Sham Stimulation — Sham stimulation will be delivered using the same coil orientation and acoustic cues as the active conditions, but with the magnetic field output disabled. Each session includes 18 minutes of sham rTMS and 3 minutes of sham TBS over the left DLPFC (F3 site). Sessions are administered 5 days per week for 4 weeks (total 20 sessions).
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07919587

SUMMARY:

## NCT07919600
Evaluation of the Effectiveness of Pulsed Electromagnetic Field Therapy in Patients With Symptomatic Plantar Calcaneal Spurs: A Prospective, Randomized, Sham-Controlled Trial

Intervention: : Active Pulsed Electromagnetic Field Therapy (PEMF) — Active pulsed electromagnetic field (PEMF) therapy will be administered using a BTL-4000 magnetotherapy device with a solenoid applicator. The treatment parameters will be a magnetic field intensity of 2 mT, a frequency of 50 Hz, and a session duration of 20 minutes. Treatment will be provided three times per week for four consecutive weeks, totaling 12 sessions. Participants will also receive a standardized plantar fascia and gastrocnemius-soleus stretching exercise program.
: Sham Pulsed Electromagnetic Field Therapy (Sham PEMF) — Sham PEMF treatment will be administered using the same BTL-4000 magnetotherapy device, solenoid applicator, patient positioning, and session duration as the active PEMF group, but without activation of the electromagnetic field output. Each session will last 20 minutes, three times per week for four consecutive weeks, totaling 12 sessions. Participants will also receive the same standardized plantar fascia and gastrocnemius-soleus stretching exercise program.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07919600

## NCT07919626
Rewiring the Chronic Pain Experience: Feasibility and Pilot Testing of a Single-Session, 4-Hour Delivery Model of Pain Reprocessing Therapy (PRT) at The Ottawa Hospital (TOH)

Intervention: : PRT (Pain Reprocessing Therapy) — The intervention involves a single 4-hour PRT in-person group session (up to 20 patients per group) at The Ottawa Hospital Pain Clinic, delivered by a PRT certified clinician.

The intervention is a brief mindfulness exercise (mindfulness of breath, energy, emotions, thoughts, sensations), followed by 90 minutes of didactic content focused on pain science education and foundation of pain reprocessing therapy. This includes interactive discussions, self-reflection, and skills building. This is followed by a brief exercise called predictive coding where participants are invited to draw a picture representing their pain. The remainder of the session includes gentle movement, somatic tracking, and practices to increase positive affect induction- specifically leaning into positive or neutral sensations and establishing short-term safety behaviours.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07919626

SUMMARY:

## NCT07919769
Closed Surgical Technique Combined With i-PRF for the Treatment of Isolated Intrabony Periodontal Defects; a Randomized Clinical Trial

Intervention: : Access Flap — Procedure/Surgery: Access Flap Intrasulcular incisions at the teeth adjacent to the defect, using the modified papilla preservation technique (MPPT). Granulation tissue is excised and debridement via hand and power-driven instruments follows. Autologous injectable platelet-rich fibrin (i-PRF) is prepared by centrifugation and then injected into the periodontal intrabony defect as an autologous bioactive adjunct to surgical treatment. Flaps are repositioned and papilla are sutured with horizontal internal mattress doubled by a single interrupted interdental suture.
: Close Surgical Technique — Strictly intrasulcular incisions are performed at the teeth adjacent to the defect (mid-buccal to mid-lingual) without incising the interdental papilla. Full thickness gingival flaps, at the base of the papilla, which is retained intact, are elevated in a "closed"-tunneling manner, granting access to the interdental osseous defect. Debridement is performed via power-driven tips and mini curettes, without intentionally excising the granulation tissue that lines the osseous defect. Autologous injectable platelet-rich fibrin (i-PRF) is prepared by centrifugation and then injected into the periodontal intrabony defect as an autologous bioactive adjunct to surgical treatment. Flaps are repositioned by gentle pressure and suturing is not required.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07919769

SUMMARY:
